# Candidate component identity triage

**Review date:** 2026-10-26  
**Input:** `candidate_dataset_staging\components.csv`  
**Purpose:** Identify records that warrant evidence review before any possible
catalog expansion. This report does not modify candidate records or authorize
integration.

## Summary and limits

The file contains 671 component rows and 14 category values. Using normalized
category + brand + model + product variant as the candidate identity gives
**182 distinct identity strings**, of which 116 occur more than once.
Normalizing only category + brand + model gives 110 identity strings across
the initially prioritized hardware categories; product-variant strings
increase that to 148 full identity strings.

This is a count of distinct strings, not a count of verified products. Every
one of the 671 candidate `source_url` values has an ID-shaped
`/products/comp_*` path rather than a manufacturer part number or product
name. The spot-checked URLs did not provide usable item-specific evidence.
**Exact candidate identities with item-level credible evidence: 0.**
Manufacturer pages consulted below
corroborate a few product-family facts and expose specific discrepancies, but
do not establish exact partner-board, motherboard, memory-kit, storage, PSU,
or case SKUs in the candidate rows.

| Review status | Rows | Meaning in this report |
| --- | ---: | --- |
| `VERIFIED_IDENTITY` | 0 | No complete candidate model/variant met this threshold. |
| `NEEDS_SOURCE_CHECK` | 61 | A relatively specific model/family label is present, but its exact source/SKU and specifications still need checking. |
| `IDENTITY_CONFLICT` | 226 | Same normalized identity has conflicting specifications or a manufacturer source contradicts a claimed identity/specification. |
| `UNRESOLVED` | 384 | The name/variant is too generic to distinguish the exact product, or repeated records cannot be established as separate SKUs. |

### Duplicate and variant assessment

- There are **zero byte-for-byte duplicate CSV rows** because IDs and source
  URLs differ.
- **83** normalized full-identity groups have identical specification JSON
  across **400** rows. This is 317 excess records beyond one row per such
  identity. They may be repeated product records; distinct IDs/placeholder
  URLs do not establish distinct SKUs.
- **33** normalized full-identity groups have different specification JSON
  across **205** rows. That is 172 excess rows beyond one per group, but does
  not establish which row or specification is correct.
- In the prioritized categories, there are 148 full-identity strings:
  58 repeated same-spec groups cover 222 rows, and 25 repeated conflicting
  groups cover 115 rows. The 402 prioritized rows reduce to 110
  brand/model strings before product variants are counted.
- Capacity labels (such as 500 GB versus 1 TB), form factors, and wattages can
  represent legitimate variants in real catalogs. Here, exact manufacturer
  model numbers/part numbers are absent or generic; therefore, the number of
  genuine distinct variants cannot be determined. No records were deleted,
  merged, or relabeled.

## Sources actually consulted

1. [NVIDIA GeForce RTX 4090 official product page](https://www.nvidia.com/en-us/geforce/graphics-cards/40-series/rtx-4090/) states 24 GB G6X memory for the GeForce RTX 4090. The candidate labels 16 RTX 4090 rows as 8 GB. This corroborates a GPU-family specification discrepancy; it does not identify the partner-board SKU.
2. [G.SKILL Ripjaws V product-family page](https://www.gskill.com/products/1/165/184/Ripjaws-V) identifies Ripjaws V as a G.SKILL DDR4 product family. Eight candidate rows attach that family name to Kingston, Corsair, or Crucial.
3. [CORSAIR DDR5 memory page](https://www.corsair.com/us/en/c/memory/ddr5-ram) describes CORSAIR Vengeance DDR5. Twelve candidate rows attach “Vengeance DDR5” to G.SKILL, Crucial, or Kingston instead of CORSAIR.
4. [ASUS PRIME B650-PLUS official product page](https://www.asus.com/motherboards-components/motherboards/prime/prime-b650-plus/) provides an example of a specific ASUS B650 model and its AM5/DDR5 specification. It does **not** substantiate the candidate's generic “ASUS B650 PRO” label; it is cited to show why exact board naming matters, not as a match.
5. [Intel ARK lookup for SKU 134587](https://www.intel.com/content/www/us/en/products/sku/134587/intel-core-i5-12400f-processor-18m-cache-up-to-4-40-ghz/specifications.html) resolved to an Intel Core i5-12400F page path. This does not support the candidate label `Intel Core i7-12400F` (`COMP_CPU_020`); recheck the intended SKU rather than silently changing the label.
6. A direct AMD product-page request for a representative CPU timed out, so no AMD-specific candidate identity/specification is treated as verified.

The candidate's own manufacturer-looking and seller-looking links were also
spot-checked: `https://www.msi.com/products/comp_gpu_001` returned HTTP 403;
`https://www.motherboard-support.com/comp_motherboard_001` failed DNS
resolution; and `https://www.vedantcomputers.in/product/comp_cpu_001` failed
at the transport layer. These failures do not prove every route is
nonexistent, but they provide no usable product evidence. The common
ID-shaped URL pattern across all records is not a product identifier or
verification.

## High-priority findings

- **GPU memory claims:** `COMP_GPU_003`, `COMP_GPU_007`, `COMP_GPU_011`,
  `COMP_GPU_015`, `COMP_GPU_019`, `COMP_GPU_023`, `COMP_GPU_027`,
  `COMP_GPU_031`, `COMP_GPU_035`, `COMP_GPU_039`, `COMP_GPU_043`,
  `COMP_GPU_047`, `COMP_GPU_051`, `COMP_GPU_055`, `COMP_GPU_059`, and
  `COMP_GPU_063` claim GeForce RTX 4090 with 8 GB. NVIDIA's official
  reference page states 24 GB G6X. These rows also belong to repeated GPU
  identity groups with divergent specification JSON; exact add-in-board
  model/SKU and correct specs need review.
- **Memory family/brand conflicts:** Ripjaws V rows under non-G.SKILL brands:
  `COMP_RAM_003`, `COMP_RAM_004`, `COMP_RAM_005`, `COMP_RAM_006`,
  `COMP_RAM_009`, `COMP_RAM_013`, `COMP_RAM_014`, `COMP_RAM_016`.
  Vengeance DDR5 rows under non-CORSAIR brands:
  `COMP_RAM_017`, `COMP_RAM_019`, `COMP_RAM_020`, `COMP_RAM_021`,
  `COMP_RAM_023`, `COMP_RAM_024`, `COMP_RAM_025`, `COMP_RAM_028`,
  `COMP_RAM_029`, `COMP_RAM_030`, `COMP_RAM_031`, `COMP_RAM_032`.
  The brand/series discrepancy means the exact identity is not supported.
- **CPU label:** `COMP_CPU_020` is `Intel Core i7-12400F`, while Intel ARK
  lookup resolves SKU 134587 to a Core i5-12400F page path. Confirm the exact
  intended model and SKU. The other CPU labels are specific enough for
  manufacturer lookup but have only ID-shaped candidate URLs; 13 AMD records
  additionally combine `socket=AM5` with `memory_support=DDR4`, which needs
  authoritative specification review.
- **Repeated conflicting specifications:** Within exact normalized
  category/brand/model/variant identities, 20 GPU groups (60 rows), five PC
  case groups (55 rows), four CPU cooler groups (45 rows), and four mouse
  groups (45 rows) carry different specification JSON. Group membership and
  record-level assignments appear in the complete triage below. Determine
  whether a real variant was omitted from the identity string or the
  specifications conflict; do not collapse those rows.
- Motherboards are generic chipset-family labels such as “ASUS B650 PRO”;
  SSD/HDD rows lack an exact product family/model; PSU and case rows use
  generic product descriptions. They cannot be safely distinguished as
  exact manufacturer products from the current fields.

## Is a useful subset available?

There is **no immediately verifiable subset of complete component identities**
ready to copy into the production catalog. The 61 `NEEDS_SOURCE_CHECK` rows
are the best candidates for targeted manufacturer lookups, not verified
records. A future useful subset may emerge after exact manufacturer
part/model numbers are supplied and the identity/specification conflicts are
resolved. Do not infer a useful integration count from the row total, repeated
IDs, or broad product families.

## Safe next step

Keep the candidate data separate. Ask the dataset provider for manufacturer
part numbers and the original item-specific specification/source references
for the 57 `NEEDS_SOURCE_CHECK` rows first, then review conflicts and
duplicates against those sources. In a separate staging copy only, normalize
and map approved records to the production schema, retain provenance, avoid
ID collisions, and test all references. Preserve separate price,
compatibility, and benchmark evidence; retain the three compatibility states
(`compatible`, `incompatible`, `unknown`); do not merge benchmark results into
editorial tiers. The prior build/upgrade examples are not evidence for ML
performance. No integration or production change is made here.

## Per-record disposition

Each row below retains its candidate ID and original source URL. “Fields to
verify” are requests for source review, not claims that those fields are
wrong. All status assignments are triage judgments only.

## Protection and verification

- Production project has no `.git` directory; Git status is unavailable.
- Production files and all seven staging CSVs were SHA-256 hashed before this
  report was written. After the required checks, all 22 existing project files
  in the fresh baseline (excluding the staging folder, virtual environment,
  Python caches, and this new report) still matched; there were zero hash
  differences.
- All seven staging CSVs also retain their original before/after SHA-256
  hashes. No candidate input was edited.
- `.\.venv\Scripts\python.exe -m unittest discover -s tests -v` — **68 tests
  passed**.
- `.\.venv\Scripts\python.exe validate_data.py` — **passed**; production
  data validated at 126 components and 25 price observations.
- No integration, production edits, or model training was performed.
